#2960. Warning to NFL general managers: Howie Roseman is about to fleece you

Warning to NFL general managers: Howie Roseman is about to fleece you originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

A broken-down receiver, a washed-up guard and an underachieving defensive tackle walk into a bar. And Howie Roseman walks out with two 1st-round picks and three 2nd-round picks.

If I were an NFL general manager, I wouldn't even answer the phone when Howie calls. Because you're going to get fleeced.

And if there's one thing to feel good about during this miserable season to nowhere, it's the way Howie has stockpiled premium draft picks with a series of what sure seem like lopsided trades.

It all started in June when Roseman shipped disgruntled and divisive A.J. Brown to the Patriots for a 1st-round pick in 2027 and a 5th-round pick in 2027.

Brown was one of the best in the league not too long ago. He owns the two-best receiving seasons in Eagles history – 88-for-1,496 and 11 TDs in 2022 and 106-for-1,456-7 in 2023.

But it sure seems like his knees won't allow him to be that kind of player again. The A.J. Brown we saw hobbling around and dropping passes in his final game in an Eagles uniform, the playoff loss to the 49ers in January, seems to be closer to the player he is now than the Pro Bowler of a few years ago.

The Patriots got a WR whose balky knees have allowed him to play only three games so far this year, with just nine catches for 101 yards, three drops and a fumble.

Of 95 WRs who've been targetted at least 20 times so far this year, Brown's 15 percent drop percentage is highest. And his catch percentage is 89th. Maybe he gets healthy the second half of the season and reverts back to that three-time Pro Bowler. Or maybe this is just who A.J. Brown is right now. Either way the Eagles got a 1st-round pick for a guy who had no future in Philadelphia, didn't want to be here and may be a shot fighter at 29.

Then came the Cam Jurgens trade, and it's still hard to believe the Ravens donated the Eagles a 2nd-round pick for a guy who just hasn't been the same player as he was in 2024. Jurgens was a tremendous interior lineman before his career was derailed by injuries. Although he made a second straight Pro Bowl last year, he was only a shell of the player he was in 2023 and 2024. This year, he struggled both at center and guard before Howie someone finagled a 2nd-round pick out of the Ravens for him.

How has it gone in Baltimore for Jurgens? You always take Pro Football Focus grades with a grain of salt, but out of 89 interior linemen who've played at least 300 snaps his 43.9 combined grade with the Eagles and Ravens is 4th-worst.

In Jurgens' three games in Baltimore, the Ravens – with both Lamar Jackson and Tyler Huntley hurt – have scored 33 points, gone 0-3, losing 35-13 to the Bengals, 36-10 to the Bills and 33-10 to the Jaguars after starting the season 4-1.

Then there's Jordan Davis. He was a good player here and at times a very good player. He probably should have made the Pro Bowl last year. But overall? As the 13th pick in the 2022 draft and the 10th-highest paid interior lineman in the league? He's underachieved, in particular this year. He was a key starter on two Super Bowl teams but he just never dominated the way you wanted him to, the way he's being paid to. And he played just 52 percent of the snaps in his nine games this year, which ranks 27th among starting interior linemen so far this year.

Great guy, natural leader, inspiring backstory, solid lineman. But hardly indispensable. Not when you also have Jalen Carter under contract and Moro Ojomo now in line for an offseason extension. The Eagles don't really have a third high-level rotational defensive tackle now – Ty Robinson, a 4th-round pick last year, will likely take over the role. But if you can get 1st- and 2nd-round draft picks for essentially a part-time player that's a no-brainer.

Add it up and Roseman got 1st-round picks in 2027 and 2028, 2nd-round picks in 2027, 2028 and 2029 and a couple 2027 5th-round picks to boot for a beat-up wide receiver, a fading interior lineman and a defensive tackle who hasn't lived up to expectations.

The way Roseman drafts, most of those picks are going to turn into important pieces as the Eagles build back toward respectability.

There hasn't been a whole lot to celebrate on the field this year, and everything points to more of the same over these last eight games.

But up on the second floor of the Jefferson Health Training Complex, Roseman is doing all he can to make sure it doesn't happen again.

#2958. Collin Sexton has focused on patience as he tries to find role with Lakers

Over the past two games, Collin Sexton has been indispensable in powering the Los Angeles Lakers to wins with their star guards, Luka Dončić and Austin Reaves, each missing one contest.

Against the Detroit Pistons on Sunday, the veteran guard tallied 17 points (5-of-10 shooting), two assists and two rebounds. A day later, he followed that up with 18 points (6-of-11 shooting), six assists, two rebounds and one steal in Monday's win over the Atlanta Hawks.

If those consecutive performances are any indication, Sexton could be finding his footing after a slow start to 2026-27, which featured a calf strain that hampered him during training camp and preseason.

Through his first nine appearances, he averaged 8.7 points while shooting 40% from the floor and 25.8% from deep (49.5% true shooting). In this week's pair of victories, he's averaged 17.5 points on 52.4% shooting and 50% from deep (64.4% true shooting). Following those laudatory outings, Sexton said he feels he's inching closer to fully being himself again.

"I think maybe another week and a half, two weeks and I think I'll be back," he told reporters Monday. "Right now, these last two games felt really good. So I'm moving in the right direction."

As Sexton works to rediscover his rhythm, he's embracing patience.

"That's one word I've been using each and every day," he said. "I would say each and every day is going to have its ups, have its downs but I'm blessed to be healthy enough to go out."

Head coach JJ Redick is counting on Sexton to help steady second units for the Lakers, given he's their most talented scorer and qualified initiator off the bench. After Monday's win, he spoke about Sexton's role in keeping the offense humming when the speedy guard is at the forefront.

"I think the biggest thing [is] we're trying to get organized and and there's glimpses of really good organization when he's out there as the primary ball handler," Redick told reporters. "We got a little squirrely there at the end of the second quarter. I thought, for the most part, outside of the first few minutes in that third quarter where I had to call the early timeout, I thought we did a good job of just being organized and trying to get good shots. That's how you end up with 124 points against a good team."

#2955. Federal Reserve Board finalizes changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 9:00 a.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Wednesday finalized changes to enhance the transparency and public accountability of its stress test and reduce volatility in its stress test-related capital requirements. The two final rules are largely similar to the proposed rules from 2025. The Board also requested comment on a proposal to better capture differences in banks' business models to generate fee income.

The Board conducts stress tests to ensure that large banks are sufficiently capitalized and able to lend to households and businesses even in a severe recession. In December 2024, the Board announced it would modify the test to improve its resilience.

"The stress test is an essential component of our regulatory capital framework," said Vice Chair for Supervision Michelle W. Bowman. "Today's changes preserve its resilience by ensuring that it is transparent, granular, and risk-sensitive. The public will now have greater assurance that the risks banks take will be reflected appropriately in their stress test losses and their capital requirements."

The first final rule requires the Board to invite public input annually on the stress test scenarios and any material model changes. It also updates the framework that guides the design of the hypothetical scenarios and adopts the models that will be used for the 2027 stress test. In addition, the final rule makes several adjustments to the stress test calendar. The final rule also updates the global market shock component, which is a set of shocks to specific market components applied to banks with large trading books. These banks will now be tested against two global market shock components each year, and the Board will use the shock that produces the largest losses for each firm to calculate the firm's stress test results.

The second final rule requires the Board, when calculating stress capital buffer requirements, to average the results from the two most recent annual supervisory stress tests for firms subject to the stress test in both years. The Board will begin averaging stress capital buffer requirements in 2028 to ensure that only models incorporating public input are used in the calculation.

The proposal would request public comment on a revision to the Board's noninterest income model that would improve the stress test's ability to capture business model diversity across firms. If adopted, these adjustments would replace the current noninterest income model that projects each bank's fee income under stress. Comments are due 60 days after publication in the Federal Register.

Together, the changes are likely to reduce year-over-year volatility in capital requirements by approximately 50 percent and are not expected to materially affect aggregate capital requirements.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call 202-452-2955.

Federal Register notice: Modifications to the Capital Plan Rule and Stress Capital Buffer Requirement

#2953. What we learned as Domantas Sabonis has uncharacteristic game in Kings' loss

What we learned as Domantas Sabonis has uncharacteristic game in Kings' loss originally appeared on NBC Sports Bay Area

SACRAMENTO — The Kings still search for their first home win of the 2026-27 NBA season after falling to the Orlando Magic by a final score of 107-100 on Tuesday night at Golden 1 Center.

The Magic, even without their best player as Paolo Banchero remains sidelined with a right ankle sprain, picked up just their third win of the season against Sacramento.

As we've seen far too often this season, the Kings' slow start to the game wound up haunting them. Sacramento turned the ball over eight times in the first quarter, resulting in 15 of the Magic's 34 points.

The Kings responded with an 18-0 run through the second quarter, but the Magic never let up and fought all the way back to regain the lead and maintain it until the final buzzer.

Zach LaVine led the way for the Kings with 31 points on 10-of-16 shooting and 6 of 9 from 3-point range, with three rebounds, two assists and two steals, but five turnovers in 33 minutes.

Kings rookie Darius Acuff Jr. trailed just behind him with 29 points on 10-of-21 shooting from the field and 4 of 9 from downtown, with four rebounds, six assists, two steals and four turnovers in 40 minutes.

Keegan Murray came off the bench for the second consecutive game after returning from an ankle injury. He finished with 11 points on 4-of-9 shooting, with two rebounds, one assist and two turnovers in 24 minutes.

Malik Monk played just three minutes off the bench. Rookie guard Emanuel Sharp played 19.

Precious Achiuwa started for the Kings but left the game early and didn't return with a leg contusion. LaVine also went to the locker room after rolling his ankle but returned.

The Kings, who dropped to 1-10 on the season, continue their seven-game homestand against the Portland Trail Blazers on Friday, when they will continue their search for their first home victory.

Here are the takeaways from the Kings' loss:

Darius Acuff Jr. entered Tuesday's game leading the NBA in minutes played per game at just above 37.

Coach Doug Christie was asked about it pregame, and he admitted Acuff's minutes should be "a tick" under where they currently are, but said he enjoys watching him play and seeing him on the floor.

On Tuesday, Acuff played 40 minutes, finishing with 29 points on 10-of-21 shooting from the field and 4 of 9 from downtown, with four rebounds, six assists, two steals and four turnovers.

The rookie really has become one of the few consistent bright spots for the Kings this season.

After a rocky start to this game, the Kings got going a bit toward the end of the first quarter.

The momentum continued into the second quarter, as Sacramento's offense came alive with an 18-0 run that began with Keegan Murray's first 3-pointer of the season.

Zach LaVine was a massive part of that run — on both ends of the floor. He had 13 points in that stretch before being substituted out of the game.

Orlando went on a run of its own with LaVine on the bench, responding with an 8-0 run to get right back in this game and eventually regain the lead.

LaVine was thrown back into the game after about two minutes of rest on the bench.

After scoring four points in the opening quarter, LaVine dropped 11 points in the second. He was plus-8 in the first half in plus/minus rating, while every other Kings starter was minus-7 or minus-8.

After a brief injury scare, LaVine finished with a team-high 31 points on 10-of-16 shooting and 6 of 9 from 3-point range, with three rebounds, two assists, two steals and five turnovers in 33 minutes.

Sabonis returned to action Tuesday after missing Sunday's game against the Miami Heat with left knee soreness.

But it was an unusual showing from the double-double machine.

He went three quarters without attempting a single shot, and his first attempt came from beyond the arc after 22 minutes of playing time. That isn't entirely shocking, as Sabonis is averaging just 9.0 field-goal attempts this season.

But what was a bit more concerning were his rebound numbers.

Sabonis, who is averaging an impressive 14.6 rebounds per game this season and nearly 11 throughout his 11-year NBA career, had just two rebounds on Tuesday.

He finished with zero points, two rebounds and five assists in 24 minutes.

Perhaps his knee still was bothering him, but nonetheless, it was an uncharacteristic showing from the rebounding machine.

#2952. Jordan Davis trade grades: Eagles celebrated, Jaguars dragged

Jordan Davis trade grades: Eagles celebrated, Jaguars dragged originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The widely expected Eagles sell-off began Monday afternoon, when they shipped defensive tackle Jordan Davis to the Jacksonville Jaguars for a first-round draft pick in the 2027 draft, as well as a second-round pick in the 2029 draft. Considering Davis' underwhelming play this season and his exorbitant price tag, the return Eagles executive VP/GM Howie Roseman got for Davis was impressive.

But what did those who cover the NFL at large think about the deal? We culled opinions from several sites to find out.

Daniel Popper: Senior NFL Writer, The Athletic – Eagles A+, Jaguars D-

"This was a significant overpay for general manager James Gladstone and the Jaguars. Davis is a very good run defender, but he does not offer nearly enough as a pass rusher to command this type of return."

"This is an impressive return for Roseman, and the Eagles are now loaded with future draft capital. They now have two first-round picks in 2027, and two first-round picks in 2028, their own and the Patriots' pick from the A.J. Brown trade. They also own two second-round picks in 2029. That is a ton of ammunition to use in whatever fashion Roseman sees fit. They could stick and pick. They could trade any of those picks for veteran players. They could package them to move around in the draft. As our Jonathan Jones wrote in our trade deadline live blog, "What the Eagles have now is total optionality."

"Roseman is doing the right thing, turning a losing season into future opportunity."

Garrett Podell: NFL Writer, CBS Sports – Eagles A, Jaguars B-

"Jacksonville's timing is right, but the price they paid for Davis is not. A first-round pick and a second-round pick for Davis isn't all that different from what the Chicago Bears gave up (2027 and 2028 first-round picks plus a 2027 fourth-round pick) for All-Pro defensive tackle Jeffery Simmons, the best defensive tackle in football at the moment. Sure, the Jaguars were never going to get Simmons since he was on an AFC South division rival, the Tennessee Titans, but couldn't they have gotten Davis for a slightly cheaper price?"

"Roseman… recouped first-round and second-round draft capital in the deal. He couldn't have asked for anything more, and he'll be able to rebuild the Eagles in no time with 10 picks in the first two rounds of the draft across the next three years."

Kevin Patra: NFL.com Senior News Writer – Eagles A, Jaguars C+

"The Jags' massive move for nose tackle Jordan Davis comes with huge risk for the GM who took the F-them picks mantle from his former boss, Les Snead, and sped away. If it works out and Davis plays a pivotal role in the Jags bringing a Lombardi to Duval, no one will give a hoot how much it costs. If it backfires, Davis struggles and Jacksonville bows out early, it will be a tough pill to swallow."

"I'm not sure Howie Roseman wanted to move Davis after just paying him, but when you get a desperate trade partner offering the moon, sometimes you just take their cash and hightail it out of there. This feels akin to the reaction when the Raiders initially got two first-round picks from the Ravens for Maxx Crosby before the trade was scuttled."

Seth Walder: NFL Analytics Writer, ESPN – Eagles A, Jaguars F

"There's no other way to start this: This trade is a disaster for Jacksonville.

"The Jaguars are absolutely right to go for it. Entering Monday, they had a 12.8% chance to win the Super Bowl, per ESPN's Football Power Index, second highest of any team and best in the AFC. And that's why I was OK with them rolling the dice on Jessie Bates III earlier in the day. A late-round pick for Davon Godchaux, too? Fine. But first- and second-round picks for Davis? Reckless would be putting it mildly."

"With Philadelphia's season effectively over, it's easy for the Eagles to unload their players. But at this kind of price, I'm inclined to think the Eagles would have dealt Davis no matter their contention status or depth chart. Philadelphia should be thrilled at this return."

#2951. The 'debasement trade' returns after Bessent bond maneuver. Crypto and gold are back in style

The debasement trade is gaining new traction on Wall Street as concern over the size and cost of the budget deficit mounts.

The conventional wisdom behind the trade is that perceived hard assets, like cryptocurrencies and precious metals, gain as investors try to hedge against a weaker U.S. dollar and Treasury debt in the face of ballooning government spending. Those fears reached a fever pitch last week before, and after, the Treasury Department's unusual step of increasing debt buybacks under Secretary Scott Bessent.

"The size of the Treasury purchases announced so far by Bessent are trivial in comparison to the size of the overall market, but the [signaling] effect was very powerful," said Stephen Coltman, head of macro at 21Shares, a crypto-focused creator of exchange-traded funds.

Gold touched three-month highs Monday, building on last week's advance of more than 5%. The yellow metal has climbed for five straight weeks and in August is on track for its biggest monthly rise since 1999.

Bitcoin added 2% Monday, to the highest since May. Last week, the digital currency soared 22%, its biggest three-day rally since 2023. Overnight Tuesday, the crypto touched $80,000.

The U.S. dollar index, which tracks the dollar against six other leading currencies, hit three-month lows last week and recorded its third down week in the last four. The index was little changed on Monday as investors expressed little appetite to bid up the greenback.

The Treasury Department said last week that it would double the maximum size of its bond buyback, to at least $4 billion from $2 billion. Two senior Treasury officials told CNBC Monday that the department could use its General Account to help fund the plans.

Last week's announcement followed news that the monthly U.S. budget deficit in July reached a five-year high and came at the same time as total federal government debt topped $40 trillion. Bessent told CNBC last week that he wields a "big toolkit" to calm the government bond market amid concerns about the government's financial health.

Long-dated U.S. Treasury yields surged last week, at one point sending the 30-year yield to almost a 20-year high of 5.34%, up from 4.82% in late June. Yields dipped and then rebounded in the wake of the Treasury buyback move, a signal that bond investors believed Bessent's moves were inadequate.

"Markets are saying something," the billionaire philanthropist and former energy trader John Arnold said in a Friday post on X. The weaker dollar, lower Treasury prices and strengthening hard assets, are "all part of the debasement trade," he wrote.

The Treasury's moves could aid the bond market, but also translate into outsized pain for the dollar, according to Nohshad Shah, Citadel Securities' head of fixed income sales for Europe, the Middle East and Africa.

The stakes are high for monetary policymakers, as a weaker greenback can ease financial conditions, Shah said. U.S. inflation — above the Federal Reserve's 2% target for five years — could worsen.

The Fed might need to hike interest rates in response, Shah said. Fed funds futures reflect about a 56% chance the central bank raises borrowing costs at its October meeting, up more than seven percentage points from a week ago, according to CME's FedWatch tool.

"The bond market's message is straightforward: fiscal or monetary policy should be tighter," Shah said in a Monday note. "Households may ultimately pay for policymakers' unwillingness to fix the roof whilst the sun is shining."

Coltman at 21Shares said the appetite for alternative stores of value also stems from mounting geopolitical tension. The U.S. on Monday rolled out a global sanctions program focused on segregating Iran from the global economy, days after slapping 50% tariffs on billions of dollars in Canadian exports.

Still, assets that would benefit from a debasement trade have recently received votes of confidence on Wall Street.

Deutsche Bank analyst Michael Hsueh said in a Monday note that gold could surpass his target price of $4,800 an ounce. Already, that level suggests the precious metal only need to rise another 3% or so from where it closed on Friday. "We see the Treasury policy change as underlining the gold constructive view," Hsueh said.

Billionaire investor Ray Dalio recommended investors remain overweight gold and bitcoin ahead of what he warned could become a debt crisis in the U.S. as a result of unchecked government spending. Gold could account for as much as 15% of a model portfolio, the Bridgewater Associates founder said.

"The government's financial condition is at an inflection point," Dalio wrote on LinkedIn Friday. "If this is not dealt with now, the debts will build up to levels where they can't be managed without great trauma."

Others are less sure, devaluing the notion of a debasement trade.

It's probably too early to endorse the idea unless it's clear that the Fed will go along with the Treasury Department, said Alexander Lis, investing chief at Social Discovery Ventures.

#2946. Game Preview: Phoenix aims to bounce back vs. Clippers

Who: Phoenix Suns (3-6) vs. Los Angeles Clippers (2-7)

Where: Mortgage Matchup Center — Phoenix, Arizona

The Phoenix Suns will need to have a short memory after being dominated by Victor Wembanyama and the Spurs last night.

Wemby walked into our building and dropped 40, 11, and nine blocks in 25 minutes without breaking a sweat. Spurs 122, Suns 96. It was a rough night. But the silver lining is that the starters were on the bench with 9:34 left. Because tonight they have the second of a back-to-back, and tired legs cannot be an excuse.

The mood in this fanbase right now is somewhere between "burn it down" and "it's November, relax." The Suns sit at 3-6. They're 2-2 on this seven-game homestand. The two-game win streak over Houston and Orlando either becomes a distant memory (after a loss) or a building block if they win, because then it's suddenly "they've won 3 of their last 4 games". So which is it?

Make no mistake about it… this is a MUST-WIN game for the Suns. These are the teams you need to beat.

Ingram and Beal are still out, so expect LA to run back the same five that started Sunday in their win over the Pelicans.

Mark Williams — OUT (Left Shoulder Surgery)

Devin Booker needs help, and he needs it from the starters.

Book went 10-for-17 for 32 last night. The other four starters combined for 27 points on 10-of-36 shooting. That's 27.8%. Phoenix shot 39.3% as a team. Jalen Green, Dillon Brooks and Miles Bridges have to be the second and third options tonight, or this turns into another Booker solo show that ends in a loss.

LA snapped a seven-game skid Sunday by beating New Orleans 139-123, and the bench won that game: 59 points and 14 assists from the reserves. Gradey Dick went 6-for-7. Top-5 pick Keaton Wagler hit 3 of 4 from deep in 18 minutes. Wagler also picked up five fouls. That unit is 4th in bench scoring per game, but defensively they have some flaws. Attack, attack, attack.

Darius Garland went for 24 and 10 against the Pelicans. Brook Lopez added 20. Brandon Ingram (heel) and Bradley Beal (knee) are both out tonight. Gillespie, Goodwin and Green have to make Garland work on the ball, and whoever draws Lopez has to drag him out to the perimeter, where his feet are slow.

Rebounding is going to be the key for every Suns game, as the Spurs outrebounded them by twenty, 55 to 35.

LA shot 55% from the field (46 of 84), went 16-for-38 from three, and piled up 34 assists against 11 turnovers in their win over the Pelicans. They lived at the line, too: 31 of 38 from the line while the Pelicans committed 29 fouls. The glass was basically even, 36-35 Clippers, with LA grabbing 13 offensive boards.

The starters held serve, every one of them finishing between +2 and +9. The bench blew it open. Max Strus and Gradey Dick were both +17, Kobe Sanders was +15, and Keaton Wagler was +11. LA won the fourth quarter 37-28.

Collin Gillespie, anyone? It's been a rough season for him, and boy could they use a breakout game in this one from him, not only for his confidence, but to outduel the Clippers' bench. Jordan Goodwin is back as well, so that hopefully provides a spark.

Do not take them lightly. The Clippers are 2nd in the NBA in 3PT% and 4th in bench scoring. Despite their flaws and 7-game losing streak, they are fully capable of getting hot from deep and stealing this one.

The Suns bounce back, and their offense breaks out in a win over the Clippers. We enter the 10-game mark at 4-6, with mixed reviews, but ultimately trending in the right direction, winning 3 of 4.

#2945. Justin Herbert, Khalil Mack remain with Chargers after trade deadline

The Los Angeles Chargers had an opportunity to be true sellers after a 0-8 start to the season, only to land somewhere closer to the middle.

Coach Jim Harbaugh and the Chargers haven't found much success during the 2026 NFL season, but there remains this sense of belief in each other.

That was on display again Tuesday with the decision to keep potential trade assets – such as quarterback Justin Herbert and outside linebacker Khalil Mack – on the roster.

The Chargers finished both the 2024 and 2025 seasons at 11-6, raising expectations that the team would be back in the playoff mix. Both of those players were around during that successful run and remain with the franchise to help make something out of the current season.

While expectations of a playoff appearance have nearly evaporated, here's why the Chargers likely decided to stay with their stars.

More: Winless Chargers make first move of NFL trade deadline with AFC West partner

Herbert could've been an attractive trade target for a few teams across the league, but a down season has tanked his stock and put him at the center of the Chargers' struggles.

Herbert has completed 144 of 235 (61.5%) of his passes for 1,591 yards, six touchdowns and 11 interceptions this season. He's also been sacked 31 times. Herbert suffering a finger injury in the same game he was sacked eight times this past weekend didn't help his stock much either.

Herbert's current contract would keep him with the Chargers through the 2029 season and includes a no-trade clause, according to Spotrac. If the team were serious about trading the quarterback, Herbert would've had a say in any potential destinations.

More: Chargers fail to make big moves at trade deadline despite abysmal season

Mack was likely the Chargers' best trade asset, but he remains in Los Angeles.

Both Mack and coach Jim Harbaugh stated publicly that the outside linebacker wanted to remain with the Chargers, despite the lost season. Mack signed a one-year deal during the offseason to remain with LA. It is his third consecutive one-year deal with the Chargers.

While the coach has provided fairly vague answers regarding Herbert and the job security for his offensive coordinator, Mike McDaniel, Harbaugh provided a clear stance.

"I want Khalil Mack here," Harbaugh told reporters earlier this month. "Khalil Mack wants to be here. He's one of the pillars of our team."

Mack shared a similar sentiment regarding the trade rumors and his desire to remain a Charger.

"They know how I feel, man," Mack said via The Athletic. "It's just tough with all the outside noise. And so, ultimately, I want to be here and help them win.

"Even though it's tough right now, you always see the light at the end of the tunnel."

Mack has 22 total tackles, three sacks and five tackles for a loss this season.

#2937. Only 4 NBA players have scored more points than Austin Reaves this season

The Lakers' Austin Reaves is having a breakout season, even though that story has been overshadowed by Luka Doncic's brilliance.

Only four players have scored more points than Reaves (278) this season, including Doncic (393), Victor Wembanyama (331), Shai Gilgeous-Alexander (299), and Tyrese Maxey (296).

Austin Reaves #15 of the Los Angeles Lakers reacts after a 3-point basket against the Detroit Pistons in Detroit, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

Reaves is 15th in the league in points per game.

Reaves is earning every penny of his four-year, $180 million deal he signed with the Lakers over the summer, the richest contract ever for a player who went undrafted.

Austin Reaves #15 of the Los Angeles Lakers moves the ball against the Detroit Pistons at Little Caesars Arena in Detroit on Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

#2936. CBA allows 49ers to not pay Brandon Aiyuk during two-week roster exemption

When unusual situations emerge in the NFL, it becomes a piece-by-piece exercise in figuring out the specific rules that apply. And few situations have been more unusual than 49ers receiver Brandon Aiyuk's odyssey of the past year.

He's back on the 49ers, at a salary of $13.75 million for the rest of the season. That fact pointed to the 49ers releasing him by 4:00 p.m. ET on Tuesday, in order to avoid owing him one-ninth of the total amount ($1.52 million) for Week 10.

But here's where yet another obscure wrinkle applies. Under Article 20, Section 4 of the Collective Bargaining Agreement, the 49ers owe Aiyuk nothing during his two-week roster exemption.

From the relevant provision: "If a Club obtains a roster exemption for a player under contract who does not report to his Club at least five days before the final day of Preseason Training Camp as defined in Article 23, Section 10, the player will not be entitled to preseason or regular season compensation until such exemption is removed, provided the player is given written notice of such fact upon reporting to the Club. If such notice is not given to the player, the player must be paid his Paragraph 5 Salary during his exemption."

So if the 49ers gave Aiyuk written notice that he won't be paid during the two-week roster exemption (and they surely did), they don't have to pay him. Which pushes their deadline for avoiding owing him a game check until Tuesday, November 24 — one day after the expiration of the exemption.

Why not just cut him? The 49ers are delaying the move because they can, postponing by up to two weeks his passage through waivers and, given his salary, inevitable free agency.

In the interim, he's on the team. But Aiyuk won't be reporting for work on Wednesday. He has been excused, we're told, from team activities and he's working out on his own.

The 49ers could release him at any moment. There's no reason not to do so, other than to apply the brakes to his ability to join another team.

The circumstances counter the notion that he was reinstated by the league subject to any type of agreement with the 49ers. If that had happened, the agreement surely would have included a requirement that the 49ers promptly cut him.

Which means that the NFL, after apparently working with the 49ers to delay Aiyuk's reinstatement in order to help the 49ers recover bonus money from Aiyuk, finally decided that reinstatement from the reserve/left squad list can't be delayed in order to advance a team's strategic objectives.

So, yes, Aiyuk eventually will be released. He wasn't released today because the 49ers don't owe him a game check for Week 10, or for Week 11. Eventually, when the roster exemption expires, they'll cut him.

For now, the team's delay in doing the inevitable points to a desire to keep another team from getting him, sticking it to Aiyuk, or some of both. It's petty, but the 49ers have the power to do it.

→ Baca artikel asli

#2931. Federal Reserve Board announces enforcement action against American Express Company to address, among other things, the firm’s failure to sufficiently detect and report certain suspicious activity related to money laundering

An official website of the United States Government

Official websites use .govA .gov website belongs to an official government organization in the United States.

Secure .gov websites use HTTPSA lock (LockLocked padlock icon) or https:// means you've safely connected to the .gov website. Share sensitive information only on official, secure websites.

The Federal Reserve, the central bank of the United States, provides the nation with a safe, flexible, and stable monetary and financial system.

Review of Monetary Policy Strategy, Tools, and Communications

Mergers, Acquisitions, and Other Applications

Financial Stability Coordination & Actions

Financial Market Utilities & Infrastructures

For release at 4:30 p.m. EDT Share

The Federal Reserve Board on Thursday announced an enforcement action against American Express Company to address, among other things, the firm's failure to sufficiently detect and report certain suspicious activity related to money laundering.

The Board also identified significant deficiencies in how American Express Company's enterprise-wide anti-money laundering program was implemented, in particular at the firm's subsidiary national bank. The Office of the Comptroller of the Currency, the primary federal regulator of the firm's national bank, has issued a separate enforcement action against the bank.

The Board's action will help ensure that the firm operates in compliance with all U.S. laws and regulations.

For media inquiries, please email [email protected] or call (202) 452-2955.

#2929. Top Wall Street analysts suggest these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Inflation data, earnings reports and tensions in the Middle East have been influencing the market. In this volatile backdrop, investors seeking income can bolster their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

The recommendations of top Wall Street analysts can help inform investors in their search for attractive stocks from the vast universe of dividend-paying companies. These experts assign ratings after analyzing a company's financials and ability to consistently pay dividends while also seeking capital appreciation.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Exxon Mobil has increased its dividends for 43 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.03 per share (annualized dividend of $4.12), XOM stock offers a yield of 2.6%.

In his latest research report on energy players, Morgan Stanley analyst Devin McDermott reiterated a buy rating on Exxon Mobil stock and increased his price target to $177 from $168.

The analyst raised his free cash flow estimates to reflect Q2 guidance and the oil price strip, with the largest increase noted for integrated players due to higher refining margins. Notably, the five-star analyst raised his cash flow estimates by an average of 20% for the second half of 2026 and 7% for 2027.

McDermott highlighted that while pure-play refiners have rallied on stronger margins, integrated energy stocks have lagged, creating relative-value opportunities in major North American integrated producers, including Exxon Mobil, Suncor and Cenovus Energy.

Overall, McDermott is bullish on Exxon Mobil, as he expects the company to deliver strong earnings and cash flow growth, driven by robust upstream volumes, high-value projects in Product Solutions, and more than $5 billion of additional structural cost savings.

"XOM's large global integrated operations and strong balance sheet (0.0x 2027 net leverage) make it a clear defensive play in an uncertain macro backdrop," said McDermott.

McDermott ranks No. 726 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 59% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.80%. See Exxon Mobil Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Moving on to natural gas producer Expand Energy. The company announced a quarterly base dividend of $0.575 per share to be paid on Sept. 3. At an annualized dividend of $2.30 per share, EXE offers a yield of about 2.3%.

Recently, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reaffirmed a buy rating on Expand Energy stock and increased his price target to $113 from $99. Mehta attributed his higher price target to cash flow improvement and a strong outlook for shareholder returns. He also cited the stock's attractive valuation, with an 11% free cash flow, or FCF, yield based on his 2027/2028 estimates compared with the peer average of 9%.

The five-star analyst expects Expand Energy to generate FCF per share of about $10 in FY28, up from the prior estimate of about $8 at his mid-cycle price forecast of $3.50/MMBtu Henry Hub. Mehta raised his FCF estimate to reflect an improved outlook for share repurchases, operational efficiencies, and gas pricing realizations.

Furthermore, the analyst expects the recently announced $1.25 billion Twin Eagle acquisition to enhance the competitive positioning of the combined business to expand to premium markets and capture incremental deals in the power and LNG end markets.

Mehta ranks No. 449 among more than 12,490 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.1%. See Expand Energy Insider Trading on TipRanks.

Mehta is also bullish on Diamondback Energy, an independent oil and natural gas company focused on energy reserves that are primarily in the Permian Basin in West Texas. The company recently paid a base cash dividend of $1.10 per share for the second quarter of 2026. FANG stock offers a dividend yield of 2.2%.

Mehta increased his price target on FANG stock to $220 from $212 and reiterated a buy rating, citing the company's capital-efficient volume growth as oil prices remain high amid persistent supply disruption in the Middle East.

"We remain constructive on FANG, a high-quality, pure-play Permian Basin operator that continues to drive incremental capital efficiencies and strong well productivity relative to peers," said Mehta.

Also, the analyst said that the removal of a minimum return of capital commitment gives Diamondback more flexibility in taking capital allocation decisions amid high oil price volatility and macro uncertainty.

Mehta added that Diamondback sees a favorable environment for production growth, driven by the potential need for global inventory restocking. He highlighted that during Q2 2026, FANG's production volumes of 1,018 Mboe/d exceeded the high end of the company's guidance, driven by a stronger-than-expected natural gas output from Barnett development and improved downstream gas marketing.

Overall, Mehta is optimistic about Diamondback's growth and sees more upside in the stock, given higher production estimates, solid productivity and a deepening position in the Barnett shale formation. See Diamondback Energy Financials on TipRanks.

#2927. Boston Celtics (8-2) at Philadelphia 76ers (9-2) Game #11 11/10/26

Boston Celtics (8-2) at Philadelphia 76ers (9-2)Tuesday, November 10, 20268:00 PM ETGame #11 Road #6TV: Peacock/NBCRadio: 98.5 The Sports Hub, 97.5 The Fanatic, Sirius XMXfinity Mobile Arena

The Celtics visit the Philadelphia 76ers for the first of 4 games between them this season. They will meet in Philadelphia again on February 7. They will meet twice in Boston on January 21 and March 7. This is likely the most anticipated matchup of the season so far as there are many stories to watch in this game.

First, there is the beef, or the lamb chops and gravy, or whatever you want to call what has been going on between Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown since the trade. In the preseason, as they were both warming up, they didn't even acknowledge each other. There has been a back and forth of words between them ever since. Also there is the story of Paul George returning to Philly for the first time and of Jaylen Brown and PG13 facing their former teams. There is also the story of the long time rivalry between the Celtics and 76ers and the teams who faced each other in the 2nd round of the playoffs last season.

There is also the story of the top 2 teams in the East facing off. The 8-2 Celtics are 2nd in the East with only Philadelphia (9-2) half a game ahead of them in the standings. The Celtics have won their last 3 games and the 76ers are looking for their 2nd straight win. The Celtics are 2-2 on the road while the 76ers are 4-2 at home. The Celtics won both games in the preseason. Both teams are 2-0 in games against division rivals.

The Celtics are playing in the first of back to back games. This is their 3rd back to back set this season already. They won the first game in both of their back to back sets this season. One win was at home while the other was on the road. The Celtics are 275-200 overall all time against the 76ers. They are 94-130 in games played in Philly. They tied the series 2-2 last season, with each team winning one at home and one on the road.

The 76ers made one of the biggest moves of the off season, trading Paul George to the Celtics to get Jaylen Brown. They also signed LeBron James, Dean Wade, Ariel Hukporti and Anfernee Simons as free agents. They drafted Labaron Philon, Jr with the 22nd pick in the draft. Along with Paul George, they lost Quentin Grimes, Kelly Oubre, Jr and Andre Drummond. Mike Gansey also replaced Daryl Morey as the 76ers' president of basketball operations.

After this game, the Celtics will complete their 4 game road trip with a game in Toronto in back to back games Wednesday. Then they play Dallas and Orlando at home before a West coast trip that takes them through Denver, Sacramento, Golden State, and Washington. They then play one game at home against Atlanta before playing at San Antonio and Dallas. Then it's home against Charlotte before a game at Brooklyn.

The 76ers will host Toronto and Indiana before road games at Brooklyn and Orlando. Then they host Milwaukee, Portland and Miami before games at Indiana and Houston. Then they have a 3 game home stand against Memphis, Denver and Orlando before playing at Orlando. Next they host Dallas and play at Charlotte. They will host Houston before a 5 game Western road trip.

The Celtics have been changing their starting lineup with Jordan Walsh, Hugo Gonzalez, Baylor Scheierman, and Neemias Queta getting starts along with Derrick White, Paul George, Jayson Tatum and Mitchell Robinson. The starting lineup is a work in progress for the Celtics, especially the shooting guard spot that has changed pretty much from game to game. Mike Conley got the start against the Bucks, but I'm guessing that White, Walsh, George, Tatum and Queta get the start in this one. The 76ers should start Maxey, Edgecombe, Brown, James and Embiid.

The Celtics have no one on the injury report as of this time. Chris Cenac, Jr and the Celtics 3 two way players will likely be with Maine. The 76ers have 3 players out as of now. Adem Bona and Dominick Barlow are both out due to back injuries. Ariel Hukporti is out for the season due to a torn Achilles. Joel Embiid has been struggling with plantar faciitis but is expected to play in this game.

Probable Starting MatchupsPG: Derrick White vs Tyrese Maxey

Celtics ReservesChris Cenac, JrMike Conley, JrLuka GarzaJordan WalshRon Harper, JrSam HauserPayton PritchardMitchell RobinsonBaylor Scheierman

Two Way PlayersDevin CarterDillon MitchellAmari Williams

76ers ReservesAnfernee SimonsDean WadeKentavious Caldwell-PopeJabari WalkerJustin EdwardsLebaron Philon, Jr,

2-Way PlayersTaco FallRayan RupertCaleb Love

Injuries/OutAdem Bona (back) outDominick Barlow (back) outAriel Hukporti (Achilles) out

Key MatchupsDerrick White vs Tyrese MaxeyMaxey is averaging 26.9 points, 3.7 rebounds, 5.5 assists, and 1.4 steals per game. He is shooting 48.1% from the field and 34.1% from beyond the arc. His 26.9 ppg ranks 3rd in the East. He is quick and so the Celtics will need to work hard to stay in front of him. They will need Derrick White's defense to be sharp in this one.

Paul George vs Jaylen BrownThis should be an incredible matchup to watch. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds, 4.0 assists, and 1.2 steals. He is shooting 47.7% from the field and 39.2% from beyond the arc. I expect Brown to come in with a chip on his shoulder to prove that he is the better player among Paul George, Jayson Tatum and himself. Paul George just needs to focus on playing team ball and being his best self.

Honorable MentionNeemias Queta vs Joel EmbiidEmbiid is averaging 17.9 points, 9.7 rebounds, and 4.1 assists per game. He is shooting 38% from the field and 10% from beyond the arc. The Celtics need to keep Embiid out of the paint and off the boards. Queta needs to find a way to stay out of foul trouble and to counter Embiid on defense.

Keys to the GameDefense – As always, defense is absolutely the key to winning this, and every, game. It is very true that defense wins championships. Shots don't always fall but players can always play hard on defense. The Celtics are 3rd with an offensive rating of 117.9 while the 76ers are 20th with an offensive rating of 110.3. The Celtics are 4th with a defensive rating of 106.3. The 76ers aren't far behind with a defensive rating of 107.6. The Celtics must give full out effort on defense for every minute of this game if they hope to beat the loaded 76ers team.

Rebound – Next to defense, rebounding is always a big key to winning. The key to rebounding is effort and focus. The 76ers are 19th with 43.4 rebounds per game while the Celtics are 10th with 45.0 rebounds per game. The Celtics have to fight as a team to try to grab every rebound to avoid the 76ers getting 2nd chance points because the 76ers average 14.7 second chance points per game. In order to win the possession battle, the Celtics must crash the boards and fight for every rebound.

Effort and Focus for 48 Minutes – The Celtics have to play with extra effort overall for all 4 quarters. They have to stay focused on playing basketball the right way as a team. They have to avoid getting wound up in the Tatum vs Brown and Brown vs George narratives. They have to play team ball and give all out effort to get to the basket, to defend, to rebound, to hustle after loose balls and to play hard on every possession.

Move the Ball Carefully – The Celtics play the best when they move the ball and find the best shot. They struggle when they try to do too much individually. But, they have to be careful handling the ball and making passes. The 76ers are best in the league at taking care of the ball with just 12.9 turnovers per game. The Celtics are 12th with 14.9 turnovers per game. They have to stay focused and avoid turning the ball over.

X-FactorsOn the Road – The Celtics will be on the road once again for the 3rd straight game and they will be facing a very hostile crowd. Philly fans hate the Celtics because they have pretty much owned them in recent years. They hate Jayson Tatum because the Celtics took him with Philly's pick after the 76ers traded up to take Markelle Fultz. They now have another reason to hate the Celtics and that is to back up Jaylen Brown who has been very bitter against the Celtics and his former running mate. There will likely be a lot of boos and the Celtics need to play through them. They have to shake off the distractions of playing on the road and stay focused on playing Celtics basketball.. They can use the boos as motivation to play even harder.

Coaching – Joe Mazzulla is in his 5th season as Celtics' head coach. He won a title in 2024 with a very talented team that was packed with shooting stars. Now he has to win in a different way since his personnel has changed and so far he is doing a good job. Nick Nurse is in his 9th season as a head coach overall and his 4th as the 76ers head coach. He won a title with the Raptors in 2019. Nurse came out ahead in the playoffs last season. Can Mazzulla win this round?

Officiating – Officiating is always an x-factor in every game. Every crew officiates differently. Some call it tight, others let them play. The Celtics need to adjust to how the refs are calling the game and not allow bad calls or no calls to take away their focus from playing the game. The Celtics also have to be aware of the new points of emphasis for this season and try to avoid flopping, off‑arm push‑offs, reckless closeouts, delays before free throws, and illegal screens. They have been especially calling flops and push offs and so the Celtics need to make an effort to avoid doing both.

#2926. J.J. McCarthy puts end to any Giants hip injury speculation

Two days after he suffered a hip pointer (deep bone bruise) that caused him to crumple to the ground throwing a warmup pass, miss a 14-play offensive series while getting treated in the locker room and limp through the final 16 minutes of a win against the Eagles, Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy skipped over all the "Will he or won't he play?" drama and was a full participant in Tuesday's practice.

"We practiced, we moved, we were going fast," head coach John Harbaugh said. "He looked good."

Giants quarterback J.J. McCarthy practices on Nov. 10, 2026. Robert Sabo for NY Post

McCarthy will make his first home start for the Giants on Thursday against the Commanders.

"I feel really good," McCarthy said. "Just being able to have a great team around me, so fortunate here and outside of the facility. It adapts every week to the aches and pains, and I felt like we did a really good job so far, and we've got to continue.

"The importance is to be out there as much as I possibly can. No matter what happens, no matter what narratives one can draw, it's being out there with my brothers and doing whatever I can to help the team be successful."

Rookie CB Colton Hood dislocated a finger during the opening minutes of practice. He left the field with a trainer holding his left hand and later wore a splint in the locker room.

"He was doing a pooch punt early in practice and landed on his finger and got dislocated," Harbaugh said. "So they had to go in [to the locker room], but it was a tough one to get back in, so it took a while, but he'll be fine."

McCarthy found out live at the podium that the Vikings — who traded him for a fifth-round pick in September — dealt a third-round pick to reacquire Kirk Cousins after realizing that neither Kyler Murray nor Carson Wentz would lead them to the promised land. Cousins left the Vikings in free agency a month before McCarthy was drafted in 2024.

"Kirk Cousins? He's going back," McCarthy said. "I wish him nothing but the best of luck, and I know he's loved in that building, so he'll be a great fit."

OLB Abdul Carter (knee/back) and OLB Malik Harrison (groin) were limited participants.

WR Charlie Jones (thumb), WR Darnell Mooney (chest) and RB Cam Skattebo (hamstring) were upgraded to full participants.
